\displaystyle\dot{\zeta}=-\frac{H}{\rho+P}\underbrace{\big(\delta P-c_{s}^{2}\delta\rho\big)}_{=\,\delta P_{\rm nad}}+\frac{k^{2}}{3a^{2}H}\big(\Psi-\mathcal{R}\big)\,,

(D.23)

where on the right-hand side we used the definition of the non-adiabatic pressure perturbation

\delta P\equiv\delta P_{\rm nad}+c_{s}^{2}\delta\rho
.
In the presence of more than one fluid, the total non-adiabatic pressure perturbation
\delta P_{\rm nad}

consists of two parts,
\delta P_{\rm nad}\equiv\delta P_{\rm intr}+\delta P_{\rm rel}
.
The first part is due to the intrinsic entropy perturbation of each fluid,

\delta P_{\rm intr}=\sum_{\alpha}\delta P_{{\rm intr,}\alpha}
with
\delta P_{{\rm intr,}\alpha}

as defined in eq. (
D.21
);
the second part of the non-adiabatic pressure perturbation,
\delta P_{\rm rel}
,
is due to the relative entropy perturbation
\mathcal{S}_{\alpha\beta}\equiv 3(\zeta_{\alpha}-\zeta_{\beta})

between different fluids

\displaystyle\delta P_{\rm rel}=-\frac{1}{6H\dot{\rho}}\sum_{\alpha,\beta}\dot{\rho}_{\alpha}\dot{\rho}_{\beta}(c_{\alpha}^{2}-c_{\beta}^{2})\mathcal{S}_{\alpha\beta}=-\frac{1}{2H\dot{\rho}}\sum_{\alpha,\beta}\dot{\rho}_{\alpha}\dot{\rho}_{\beta}(c_{\alpha}^{2}-c_{\beta}^{2})(\zeta_{\alpha}-\zeta_{\beta})\,.

(D.24)

From eq. (
D.15
), on the other hand, we get

\displaystyle\dot{\mathcal{R}}=\bigg(\frac{\dot{H}}{H}+3Hc_{s}^{2}\bigg)\big(\zeta+\mathcal{R}\big)+\frac{H}{\rho+P}\big(\delta P-c_{\alpha}^{2}\delta\rho\big)-\frac{k^{2}}{3a^{2}H}\,\Psi\,.

(D.25)

D.2.1
Perturbations dynamics in the Axion-curvaton model

We now interpret eqs. (
D.21
,
D.22
,
D.23
,
D.25
)
in light of the curvaton model studied in the main body of this paper.
We have two fluid species, namely the curvaton and the radiation field, that are identified, respectively, with the labels
\alpha=\phi,\gamma
. The curvaton field decays into radiation with a decay rate
\Gamma_{\phi}
, which we take to be a constant.

Consider the dynamics during phase I. We set
\Gamma_{\phi}=0
so that we do not have energy transfer between the scalar field and radiation. Furthermore we neglect the curvature perturbation and comoving curvature perturbation of the radiation. Hence the eqs.
D.17
and eqs.
D.18
simply read as

\displaystyle\zeta_{\phi}\equiv-\psi-H\frac{\delta\rho_{\phi}}{\dot{\rho}_{\phi}}=\frac{2\ \delta\theta_{0}}{3\ \theta_{0}}\,,\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ {\rm and}\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \zeta\equiv\sum_{\alpha}\frac{\dot{\rho}_{\alpha}}{\dot{\rho}}\,\zeta_{\alpha}=\frac{\dot{\rho}_{\phi}}{\dot{\rho}_{\phi}+\dot{\rho}_{\gamma}}\zeta_{\phi}\,,

(D.26)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\phi}\equiv\psi-HV_{\phi}=\frac{\delta\theta_{0}}{\theta_{0}}\frac{H\ \theta}{\delta\dot{\theta}}\,,\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ {\rm and}\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \mathcal{R}\equiv\sum_{\alpha}\frac{\rho_{\alpha}+P_{\alpha}}{\rho+P}\,\mathcal{R}_{\alpha}=\frac{\rho_{\phi}+P_{\phi}}{\rho_{\phi}+(4/3)\rho_{\gamma}+P_{\phi}}\mathcal{R}_{\phi}\,,

(D.27)

where in this case the time-dependent quantities are evaluated solving the system given by eqs. (
7.28
-
7.30
).
We can use these equations as the initial conditions for the system that describe the evolution of the perturbations during phase II and III.
For the sake of clarity, we introduced explicitly the subscript
k
to remark that perturbations are Fourier modes with fixed comoving wavenumber
k
.

Now we consider the case of phase II+III, as defined in section
7.1.2
.
The scalar field
\phi
verifies the Klein-Gordon equation of motion
\ddot{\phi}+3H\dot{\phi}+\mathcal{V}^{\prime}(\phi)=0

and its energy density and pressure are given by

\displaystyle\rho_{\phi}=\frac{1}{2}\dot{\phi}^{2}+\mathcal{V}(\phi)\,,\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ P_{\phi}=\frac{1}{2}\dot{\phi}^{2}-\mathcal{V}(\phi)\,,

(D.28)

with adiabatic speed of sound

\displaystyle c_{\phi}^{2}=\frac{\dot{P}_{\phi}}{\dot{\rho}_{\phi}}=1+\frac{2\mathcal{V}^{\prime}(\phi)}{3H\dot{\phi}}\,.

(D.29)

The energy transfer from the curvaton field to radiation is described by

Q_{\phi}=-\Gamma_{\phi}\rho_{\phi}
(and, consequently,

Q_{\gamma}=\Gamma_{\phi}\rho_{\phi}
).
Radiation is a perfect fluid with
P_{\gamma}=\rho_{\gamma}/3
.
The perturbations in the energy transfer are described by

\delta Q_{\phi}=-\Gamma_{\phi}\delta\rho_{\phi}
and

\delta Q_{\gamma}=\Gamma_{\phi}\delta\rho_{\phi}
(where, as stated before, we are assuming that
\delta\Gamma_{\phi}=0
).
Consequently, as discussed below eq. (
D.21
), we have

\displaystyle\delta Q_{{\rm intr,}\phi}=\delta Q_{\phi}-\frac{\dot{Q}_{\phi}\delta\rho_{\phi}}{\dot{\rho}_{\phi}}=0\,.

(D.30)

During this phase, we have
P_{\phi}=0
, and the scalar field behaves as a pressure-less fluid.
Consequently, we have
\delta P_{{\rm intr,}\phi}=0
.
Eq. (
D.21
), therefore, simplifies to

\displaystyle\dot{\zeta}_{\phi}=-\frac{\dot{H}Q_{\phi}}{H\dot{\rho}_{\phi}}\big(\zeta-\zeta_{\phi}\big)+\frac{k^{2}}{3a^{2}H}\bigg[\Psi-\bigg(1-\frac{Q_{\phi}}{\dot{\rho}_{\phi}}\bigg)\mathcal{R}_{\phi}\bigg]\,.

(D.31)

Using the background dynamics, and introducing the
e
-fold time as time variable,
we recast the previous equation in the form

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d\zeta_{\phi,k}}{dN}\right|_{\rm phase\,II+III}=\frac{(3+\Omega_{\gamma})\Gamma_{\phi}}{2(3H+\Gamma_{\phi})}\,(\zeta_{k}-\zeta_{\phi,k})+\frac{k^{2}}{3(aH)^{2}}\,\Psi_{k}-\frac{k^{2}}{(aH)^{2}}\frac{H}{(3H+\Gamma_{\phi})}\,\mathcal{R}_{\phi,k},

(D.32)

where the notation
\left.\right|_{\rm phase\,II+III}
remarks the fact that the corresponding evolution equation
is strictly valid during the oscillating and decaying phase.
To close the system, we need the evolution of
\zeta
,
\mathcal{R}
and
\mathcal{R}_{\phi}
(given that
\Psi
is related to
\zeta
and
\mathcal{R}
via eq. (
D.20
)).
Since radiation has a well-defined equation of state, we also have
\delta P_{{\rm intr,}\gamma}=0
.
Consequently, we find

\displaystyle\delta P_{\rm nad}\equiv\delta P_{\rm intr}+\delta P_{\rm rel}=\delta P_{{\rm intr,}\gamma}+\delta P_{{\rm intr,}\phi}+\delta P_{\rm rel}=\delta P_{\rm rel}=\frac{\dot{\rho}_{\gamma}\dot{\rho}_{\phi}}{3H\dot{\rho}}(\zeta_{\phi}-\zeta_{\gamma})=\frac{\dot{\rho}_{\phi}}{3H}(\zeta_{\phi}-\zeta)\,.

(D.33)

where in the last step we used eq. (
D.17
). Eq. (
D.23
) gives

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d\zeta_{k}}{dN}\right|_{\rm phase\,II+III}=\frac{(3H+\Gamma_{\phi})\Omega_{\phi}}{(3+\Omega_{\gamma})H}\,(\zeta_{\phi,k}-\zeta_{k})+\frac{k^{2}}{3(aH)^{2}}\,(\Psi_{k}-\mathcal{R}_{k}).

(D.34)

The evolution of
\mathcal{R}_{k}
is governed by

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d\mathcal{R}_{k}}{dN}\right|_{\rm phase\,II+III}=\bigg[\frac{1}{H}\frac{dH}{dN}+\frac{4H\Omega_{\gamma}-\Omega_{\phi}\Gamma_{\phi}}{H(3+\Omega_{\gamma})}\bigg]\mathcal{R}_{k}+\bigg(\frac{1}{H}\frac{dH}{dN}+1\bigg)\zeta_{k}-\frac{(3H+\Gamma_{\phi})\Omega_{\phi}}{H(3+\Omega_{\gamma})}\,\zeta_{\phi,k}-\frac{k^{2}}{3(aH)^{2}}\,\Psi_{k},

(D.35)

while we find for
\mathcal{R}_{\phi,k}

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d\mathcal{R}_{\phi,k}}{dN}\right|_{\rm phase\,II+III}=-\bigg(\frac{\Gamma_{\phi}}{H}+\frac{1}{H}\frac{dH}{dN}\bigg)(\mathcal{R}_{k}-\mathcal{R}_{\phi,k}).

(D.36)

The system formed by eqs. (
D.32
,
D.34
,
D.35
,
D.36
)
is subject to the relation

\displaystyle\frac{3}{H}\frac{dH}{dN}(\zeta_{k}+\mathcal{R}_{k})=\frac{k^{2}}{(aH)^{2}}\,\Psi_{k}

(D.37)

We solve numerically the evolution described by eqs. (
D.32
,
D.34
,
D.35
,
D.36
)
, that is valid during phase II and phase III (
P_{\phi}=0
), in order to get the correct value of the curvature perturbation
\zeta_{k}
for the power spectrum defined in eq. (
7.40
).
